## What a read cannot reach

Every figure so far reports what a structure *answers*. None reports what it cannot reach, and the two are different questions. The writeback only ever sees what arrived in the context, so a memory no read returns is not merely unread — it is frozen, and it stays whatever it was for as long as it exists. A decade of rows in that state shows up in no recall figure.

**Related is read off the workload and never off similarity.** The workload knows which values are true, which thread each sits on and which subject it belongs to. A relation defined by a retrieval function would measure that function rather than the structure, which is the substitution this programme exists to avoid. Two relations are reported: the values live on the thread the question is about, which is recall under another name, and the values live anywhere on that subject, which is what a turn could have corrected had the context carried it.

**The frozen share is what a structure holds that no context has ever carried**, counted in planted values so the same number means the same thing for a store of rows and a store of addresses. It is reported per year so it can be seen accumulating, and beside it is how much of it a later checkpoint still needs — which is what forgetting the never-reached would cost.

**Whether a write can reach what a read cannot** is constructed rather than argued, in `harness/coupling_test.go`: a fact is written, never once read back, and then corrected, with enough other facts in the same words that the row a lookup must find is not alone. What each structure does with that correction is the measurement.

**Capacity is measured rather than bounded.** The trajectory is reported for every year rather than three of them, and what it extrapolates to is computed from it, so "nothing approaches a limit" is a finding rather than an assumption.

**What these figures say.**

- **A write reaches what a read cannot, in one structure and not the other.** Constructed directly: a fact written, never once read back, then corrected among enough facts in the same words that a lookup does not find it alone. The addressed store applies the correction — the address is computed from the sentence, so the write lands whether or not anything ever returned that address. The store that supersedes what its own lookup surfaces does not. Reading and updating are decoupled in one design and coupled in the other, and that is a larger difference between them than any figure this record carried before.
- **The same store fails to correct even what it can find, which bounds what the coupling explains.** With the row it must correct sitting alone, a correction phrased as a correction still does not land, while a restatement in the same words does: supersession fires on how much the two sentences share, and a correction shares little with what it corrects. So two causes are present and this workload separates them only in the constructed case. The workload-scale column reads backwards for the same reason — on that store a value no read returned is usually a value some unrelated write already archived, so "never reached" and "no longer held" are close to the same event and the column is not evidence about the coupling. The test is the measurement; the column is reported so a reader is not left to find that out.
- **Nothing is frozen in any structure this project invented.** Every planted value each of those structures holds has been carried into some context at least once. For the addressed stores that is unsurprising: the address asked is the address returned, and every address is asked every year. For the shipped store it is not — it holds hundreds of rows and returns tens per turn, but a decade asks thousands of questions, so the budget cycles through everything many times over.
- **Two published designs freeze most of what they hold, which is the prediction this section made arriving.** The earlier round said the frozen share turns positive once what a structure holds outruns what a decade of contexts can carry, and named the ratio as the number to watch. `generative-agents` and `reflection` keep every turn — thousands of records against a context of a few — and by the last year four fifths and seven tenths of the planted values they hold have never once been carried into any context. The share climbs monotonically from the first year in both. What was written as a prediction about a store nobody had built is now a measured property of two published ones, and it was not a property of this workload's size after all: the same workload freezes nothing in a structure that returns the address it was asked for.
- **What is frozen is not what nothing needs.** At the ninth year `generative-agents` holds hundreds of frozen values that a later checkpoint still asks for, and `reflection` holds a comparable number. These are not stale rows a forgetting rule would be right to drop; they are facts the structure holds, cannot reach, and will be asked about.
- **The bytes that cannot be reached are the archive, and they are almost all of them.** The frozen share is zero over what a search can reach because the archive is not part of it: the shipped store never hard-deletes, no query reaches an archived row, and at the last year its stored bytes stand at two orders of magnitude above the content its active index holds. Those bytes are held, cannot be read, and cannot be corrected — the case the criterion describes, sitting outside the index rather than inside it.
- **A forgetting rule keyed on use has nothing to forget in the structures it was written for, and would gut the two that freeze.** The rule the frozen share would feed — drop what no read has ever reached — is well defined, needs no threshold and no clause for a class, and on the structures this project invented it removes nothing, because nothing qualifies. It cannot be shown safe or unsafe by a run in which it never fires. On `generative-agents` it would fire on most of the store, and the column beside the frozen one says that much of what it would take is still asked for later. The rule is not thereby refuted — a store that cannot reach a value is not answering with it either — but a rule that removes nothing in one structure and most of another is a rule whose safety is a property of the structure it runs in.
- **A structure that answers the question brings back very little else.** The addressed stores carry about an eighth of what is live about the subject they are asked about; the shipped store carries about half, because a budget filled by ranking sweeps in whatever else scores. Under the criterion this round is for, that is the addressed store's one disadvantage — everything about that subject the context did not carry is outside the reach of that turn's writeback. What the coupling result says is that for an addressed store it does not follow that those facts are unfixable, because a later correction does not need to have read them.
- **The capacity ceiling is a decade away for a structure that keeps every turn and a dozen decades away for one that keeps facts.** Every structure that holds facts adds tens of units a year, and at that rate the active-index capacity is twelve decades off or further. The two designs that keep every observation add hundreds a year and reach the same ceiling inside a second decade. Capacity is not what bounds a decade for the structures this project was choosing between; for a structure that stores the conversation rather than what it asserted, it is the next thing to bind after the reads stop reaching.

## What a constraint costs

An unbounded store makes every structure look manageable, because nothing ever has to be given up. Every figure outside this section was measured where nothing had to be discarded and nothing had to be left out of the context. The programme's question — which structure holds and updates memory over years — only becomes a comparison once something has to go.

**The capacities are chosen to bite.** The loosest sits just under what the largest structure ends the decade holding, so it evicts a little; the tightest is a small fraction of it. A bound that never fires reproduces the non-result this section exists to correct — a run in which a rule never fires says nothing about the rule.

**The rules that decide an eviction are compared the way the arity candidates were**: each measured, none tuned, and a rule needing a threshold disqualified rather than fitted. They live outside every structure, in the run, so no frame carries a clause for one and each is bound the same way. What the run knows it knows from having watched: when it handed a thing over, when a context last carried it, what salience the write would have given it, and whether the write said it carried a fact at all. Nothing is asked of a frame except what it holds and, when it is over, to give some of it up.

Five candidates. Four decide from the store's own history — oldest first, least recently read, never read, lowest salience, which is what the shipped design's own eviction approximates. The fifth is told by the write which of its own turns are disposable, the way the write tells it arity.

**The read budget is a variable rather than the constant every other figure was measured at.** It is swept from far below the shipped value to far above, and reported against the criterion this round is for: a structure whose related facts all fit inside the budget is manageable, and one whose do not is not. A structure that returns the address it was asked for and nothing more has no such budget, and that is a different shape of answer rather than a worse score.

The constrained runs use the oracle arm alone: the question is what a structure does when it must give something up, and in the text arm the addressed structures hold nothing to give. Their tables are separate from the unbounded ones throughout, and the unbounded figures are unchanged.

**What these figures say.**

- **Every structure collapses under a hard bound, and they collapse together.** At a capacity a fraction of what a decade produces, every store that holds facts answers the same small share, because what survives is what arrived last. The differences the record has spent five rounds establishing — how arity is decided, whether a write destroys, what a store holds beside the answer — do not show up at all once eviction is doing the deciding. Under constraint the eviction rule dominates the structure.
- **`carried` is what a bound throws away, and it is exactly what the programme is for.** A fact asserted once in the first year and asked in the last is the oldest thing in the store and the last thing to be re-asserted, so an age-ordered eviction takes it first. Under oldest-first at the hard bound it is answered on nothing at all, in every structure, while the year's own facts are answered in full. A store bounded that way remembers only what it has just been told, which is the failure the shipped design already has in the text arm arriving by another route.
- **A rule keyed on use saves it, and that is a real difference from the arity question.** Least-recently-read and never-read both keep `carried` almost or entirely intact at the same bound where age-ordered eviction loses all of it — because a carried fact is read at every checkpoint that asks it even though it is never re-asserted. Forgetting turns out to be decidable from what the store has watched happen, where arity was not. The two questions do not resolve the same way, and this is the round that says so.
- **What it saves it pays for out of the newest facts.** The same rules that keep `carried` give up the current year's, because what has just arrived has not been read yet and a use-ordered rule cannot tell young from unwanted. Overall recall is better than age-ordered eviction and the profile is not uniformly better; the trade is between what is old and proven and what is new and unproven, and no rule measured escapes it.
- **The write cannot say what is disposable in a store that only holds facts.** The rule told by the write scores exactly what lowest-salience scores, everywhere, because in this workload a turn carrying no fact is also the low-salience one — the two are one signal under two names. In the addressed structures it does nothing at all, because everything they hold is a fact and none of it was ever disposable. The signal is spent at the door: an addressed store already discarded everything the write would have marked.
- **Bounding the index makes the footprint larger, not smaller.** The shipped store's total bytes rise as its capacity falls, because eviction archives rather than deletes and the archive is what the bytes are. A capacity bound on that design buys a smaller searchable index and a bigger store. The structures that hold facts and nothing else fall with their bound as one would expect, and the gap between the two behaviours is the widest this record has measured.
- **The read budget buys reach long after it stops buying recall.** For the store that fills a budget by ranking, recall stops improving well before the budget stops improving what else the context carries: the related facts about the subject keep arriving as the budget grows, and the answer to the question asked stopped changing. Under the criterion this round reports against, the manageable budget is far above the shipped value and far above where recall plateaus.
- **An addressed store has no such budget, and that is the answer rather than a low score.** Its recall, its staleness and what it carries about the subject are identical at every budget from a fraction of the shipped value to four times it, because it returns the address it was asked for and nothing else. Nothing about giving it a larger context makes it bring back more. Whether that is what one wants is the same question #471 left open, and the budget sweep does not settle it.

## What harder language costs

Every figure outside this section was measured on one conversation shape, and this record's own limits say so: short single-clause sentences, one fact to a message, and an extraction the oracle performs without ever erring. Real conversation is none of the three. There is reason to expect the sentence matters rather than merely to check: §What a model declared has the same model reading a conjunction perfectly and a narrowing qualifier at well under half of that, on sentences of one clause each. If one qualifier does that, the shape of a sentence is already the dominant variable, and nothing in this programme had run past one clause.

**It is a separate run with its own workload, its own digests and its own result file.** `harness/harder.go` generates three shapes from one set of threads and one schedule, and `results/harder_seed7.json` holds what every frame answered on each. It writes its own files rather than extending the decade run's, and no row of the decade run's results moved. It is smaller than the decade run because every message is read by a model once per shape and the bound on that sets its size, so its figures are comparable to each other and to nothing in the decade run.

- `plain` — the decade run's sentences at this run's size. It is what the other two are read against, and without it a difference would have nowhere to be a difference from.
- `clausal` — the same assertions, each wrapped in a subordinate frame, so a sentence embeds and qualifies instead of stating one clause.
- `packed` — the plain sentences, three to a message, so one message carries three replace-or-join decisions.

Each varies one thing: `clausal` is the sentence and `packed` is the number of decisions in it. Whether they are genuinely harder is measured rather than asserted — §How hard the language actually is reports the characters and clauses the generator produced, so a null result can be told from a generator that produced nothing to be null about.

**The framing carries no arity marker, and a test holds it there.** `attribute-cued` decides arity by reading the whole message for one of its addition cues, so a cue in the framing is read as though the assertion had carried it. The first connective list this study was built with joined packed assertions with a phrase containing one — and it marked a large share of the packed fact-carrying messages as additions, nearly all of them for no reason but that phrase, putting `attribute-cued` at its best recall of any shape while it returned superseded values on most revised threads. It was reading the generator rather than the workload. No figure produced by that list is published: `harness/harder_test.go` now checks every phrase the generator can emit against the cue rule — it ranges over the three lists the generator draws its framing from rather than over a copy of them, so a phrase added to one of those lists is checked without the test changing — and checks that framing an assertion never changes what that rule reads out of it, and the study was re-run. It is recorded here because a reader of the `attribute-cued` rows needs to know that the sentence a frame was measured against is a thing that can be quietly edited, and what stops it.

**The middle of the extraction bracket.** This record has both ends and nothing between them: under the oracle the addressed frames answer nearly everything, and with no extraction they answer nothing at every year, because there is nowhere to put a sentence they cannot address. So a third arm runs beside those two. `extracted` is the same messages carrying what a model read out of them — one call a message, answering in JSON, the same model this record already pays for elsewhere. Every frame runs in it, on all three shapes, and it is where a buildable structure actually sits.

**The extractor is given the attribute vocabulary.** An addressed store has an address space, and what is asked here is whether a model finds the facts and puts them in the right places — not whether it can invent a set of keys and keep to it, which is a harder problem and a different one. Every misaddressing counted below is therefore a choice among names the model was handed.

**Failures are reported by kind and never as one rate.** A fact missed leaves an addressed store empty where it should have held something. A fact addressed to the wrong `(subject, attribute)` puts something where nothing belongs, which corrupts rather than omits. A fact invented from a message that asserted none fills a store with what was never said. Those are three different harms and one rate covering them would hide which of them a structure has.

**§What each frame answered on each shape reports the last year of each run.** Three shapes across three arms and every frame is already a long table, and a year column would multiply it by the span for a question that is about the sentence rather than about time. What a shape costs shows in what a structure ends the span holding and answering; the per-year form of the same question is the decade run's table.

**Whether the failures inside one message are independent is its own figure.** Three assertions in a sentence can fail three times or once, and a rate per assertion cannot tell those apart — the second is a message read wrong rather than three decisions gone wrong. So the share of messages read entirely right and the share read entirely wrong are reported beside the per-assertion rate, and what independence would predict is arithmetic a reader does on those two.

**What these figures say.**

- **Harder language barely changes what an extractor finds, and changes where it puts it.** The share of planted facts read correctly falls only slightly from plain sentences to subordinated ones to packed ones, and the share missed does not rise at all. What rises on every step is the share read out at the wrong address: none whatever on plain sentences, and highest where three assertions share a message. The harm that corrupts an addressed store rather than leaving it empty is the one the language produces, and a single extraction rate would have reported the whole thing as no change.
- **Nothing was invented out of small talk, on any shape.** Every message that asserted nothing returned nothing, in all three. What inventions there are sit inside messages that did assert something — a fact added beside the ones that were there rather than conjured from an empty message. Whatever a writeback needs guarding against here, it is not a store filled from chatter.
- **The write operation degrades further than anything else the language touches.** Of the facts the model both found and addressed correctly, the share whose replace-or-join it also read correctly falls on every step from plain to clausal to packed, and its intervals at the two ends do not overlap — where the correct rate's do. §What would be chosen today puts arity in the write, so this is the part of the design most exposed to the sentence, and it is the part harder language damages most.
- **A packed message is read whole or not at all.** The share of packed messages with every assertion right stands above the per-assertion rate raised to the assertions a message carries, by a margin its interval barely clears, and the share with every assertion wrong stands far above what the same arithmetic gives. The failures inside a sentence are not independent: it is understood or it is not, and its three decisions go with it. The same number of facts is lost either way, in fewer and larger pieces — which is a different risk to hold than three separate ones and not a smaller one.
- **An addressed store's answers do not depend on the sentence, and the one arm that reads the sentence does not move either.** In the oracle arm every attribute frame, the interval store and the log post identical rows on all three shapes, class for class and figure for figure. For all but one of them that is not a null result needing explanation: they hold an address and a value, so there is nothing about the sentence left for them to be sensitive to. `attribute-cued` is the exception — its write rule reads the whole message for an addition cue, so the sentence does reach it, and its rows are identical for the narrower reason that no framing carries such a cue, which a test holds, and that no packed message in this workload puts a cue-carrying assertion beside one without, which nothing holds. Everything harder language does to any of them has to arrive through the writeback.
- **The two frames that read text are the two the language reaches.** `shipped` and `document` are the only frames whose oracle-arm rows move between shapes at all, and `shipped` moves a long way: about a quarter of its recall goes on subordinated sentences, and on packed messages `replaced` falls to a fraction of what it answers on plain ones. Its retrieval matches a question against sentences it kept, and a sentence carrying a housekeeping preamble and two other people's facts matches worse. The design that holds text pays for the shape of the text and the designs that hold facts do not.
- **A real extractor puts an addressed structure close to its ceiling and nothing like the floor.** In the `extracted` arm every addressed frame answers most of what the same frame answers under a perfect oracle, on all three shapes, against nothing at all in the arm with no extraction. The bracket this record has carried since it opened now has a middle and the middle is near the top: the distance from an oracle to a real extractor is small beside the distance from a real extractor to none. What makes an addressed structure buildable is that something extracts, not that something extracts perfectly.
- **In that arm the shapes do not order.** The addressed frames answer no worse on the harder shapes than on the plain one — on several of them slightly better, because that pass of the extractor happened to miss fewer facts on the harder sentences. What separates the shapes is which failures the extractor makes and not how many, and one pass of one model is too thin a sample for the difference between the shapes to be read as a difference at all.
- **The ceiling stops being a ceiling once a real extractor supplies it.** `attribute-declared` answers every class at every year when the oracle hands it the declarations, and loses recall on all three shapes when a model reads them off the message. On the packed shape it also returns superseded values, where under the oracle it returns none on any shape — at a rate whose interval reaches zero, so what the table supports is that the failure appears where a message carries several decisions, not how large it is. The frame that cannot be wrong about arity is wrong about it anyway, because it was told wrong: a ceiling measured with a perfect informant bounds the structure and not the system.
- **No claim here rests on the cuts by wording or by class.** Each cut divides a few hundred assertions among six classes or four wordings, so its cells are small and their intervals are correspondingly wide; a cell that moves between shapes moves on a sample too thin to say the shape moved it. They are reported so these tables read against §What a model declared, and so a larger run has something to be read against.

## Whether a structure can find what it holds

Every figure outside this section was produced with the question already turned into `(subject, attribute)`. A frame handed the address is measured on holding and on updating; the standard this programme is measured against has a second half — surfacing the meaningful one — and no addressed structure had been measured on it. This section is the run in which no frame receives the address unless the form it is measured under supplies one.

**It is a separate run with its own file, on the decade run's workload.** `harness/finding.go` measures it and `results/finding_seed7.json` holds it. The workload is generated from the same seed with the same parameters and carries the same turn digest, so where this run hands over the address it answers what the decade run answered — which `harness/finding_test.go` checks against the committed decade file value by value rather than by hashing it. No row of that file moved.

**Three forms, and the arm is not one of them.** An arm decides what a turn carries when a frame observes it. A form decides what a question carries when a frame is asked. Both arms run under every form.

- `addressed` — the question carries the address it was generated from. It is the ceiling every figure elsewhere in this record was measured at.
- `worded` — the question carries its words and nothing else.
- `resolved` — the question carries the address a model read out of its words: one call per question, answering in JSON, the same model this record already pays for elsewhere. It is the read-side twin of the `extracted` arm, and it is where a buildable design sits.

**Nothing was added to a structure to make it find.** The recommendation is a store that holds an address and a value and reads one place, and bolting a ranking function and a threshold onto it to make a worded question work would be a different structure with a different simplicity claim — and would answer a question nobody asked. So no frame gained a scorer, and what a worded question reaches an addressed store as is nothing at all.

**A resolver that matched the question's words was not built.** Every question here is written from the attribute's own template, so the subject's name and usually the attribute's own noun stand in it as tokens. A step that matched those words against the address space would place nearly every question and would be scoring the generator rather than a design. What is measured instead is the step a system would ship — a model reading the question — and the same objection applies to its figure, which is why §Limits calls it a ceiling rather than an estimate.

**A question about a person is a partial address.** Every checkpoint before this round named one address; `What do you know about <subject>?` names a subject and no attribute. Its ground truth is every value true anywhere on that subject — the relation §What a read cannot reach already defined, read off the workload rather than off any retrieval function. An addressed structure answers it from every address under the subject, emitted in attribute order: the order carries no claim about which of them matters, because such a structure holds nothing that would support one.

**The two kinds of question are asked in separate runs over separate stores.** A read is not always free — the shipped store records an access on every recall — so asking both out of one store would answer the second from a store the first had already moved.

**What a structure returns when the relevant set does not fit is reported rather than repaired.** The read budget is swept under the multi-address question the way #472 swept it under the single-address one. A structure that fills a budget answers inside whatever budget it is given; a structure that holds an address and a value has no budget to answer inside, so what it returns is everything the partial address reaches, and the table reports how far past the budget that goes rather than cutting it to fit. Cutting it needs an ordering, an ordering is a mechanism those stores do not have, and building one to publish a number is the move this programme has refused before.

**The share inside the budget is a threshold and the multiple is not.** The shipped store bounds its own answer with a per-row token estimate that divides by four and rounds down, so its answers come back a few per cent past the budget in characters and its share-inside reads zero for a reason that has nothing to do with how much it found. The column beside it, what it returned as a multiple of the budget, is where that answer's size is read. An answer that is empty fits trivially, which is why neither column is read without the recall beside it.

**What these figures say.**

- **Nothing that holds facts at an address answers a question in words.** In the arm where they hold everything, every addressed structure — the attribute arms, the interval store, the log — posts a recall of zero at every year, on the single-address question and on the multi-address one alike, and the units-read column beside it is zero too. That is not a score to be improved. A store whose read path is a lookup has nowhere to look when the question carries no key, and what this record has been measuring for those structures all along is holding and updating.
- **The two structures that hold text are the two the form does not move.** `shipped` and `document` post identical figures under all three forms, because neither reads an address: what a question carries reaches only the family that addresses. The text arm is form-invariant for the same reason from the other side — a store that holds nothing answers nothing whatever the question carries — and both invariances are in the table rather than asserted.
- **A step in front of the store recovers all of it but one question.** With a model turning the question's words into an address, every addressed structure answers what it answers under the oracle, class for class, at every year but one. The model misplaced a single question of the several hundred it was asked, and the damage is exactly what a misplaced question does: one thread unanswered in the one year it was asked, in the class asked only once.
- **That is the easiest form of the addressing problem and the figure is a ceiling.** Every question here is written from its attribute's template, so it carries the subject's name and usually the attribute's own noun. A question that only implied what it asked about is where such a step would be tested, and none is asked here.
- **The finding does not have to live in the store, and putting it in front costs one model call a question.** The paid table below states what that came to in calls and tokens. It is the same kind of cost the write side already pays — a model reading language — once for each question instead of once for each turn, and it buys an answer from a store that still reads one place.
- **A question about a person spans several addresses by the end of the decade, and an addressed structure answers it by reading them and nothing else.** It touches the addresses under one subject where the shipped store ranks its whole active index and the log projects every event it ever kept, and the gap between those columns is the same one the single-address question opened, widened by nothing.
- **On that question the structures separate on what they return that is no longer true, not on what they find.** The shipped store finds nearly as much of a person as the addressed store that takes its arity from the write, and returns a superseded value on most subjects while doing it; the addressed store returns none. Ranking against the question's text finds a person's facts, and brings back what has stopped being true along with them.
- **A structure with no store at all answers almost nothing about a person.** The bounded document holds the most recent turns and a person's facts are spread over a decade, so the multi-address question is the one place in this record where the floor is not close to anything.
- **The addressed store's insensitivity to the read budget survives the multi-address question, and stops being free.** From the tightest budget swept to the loosest it returns the same characters: at the loosest that is a fraction of what it was allowed, and at the tightest the store that keeps every value is past what it was allowed on half the subjects. #472 read that insensitivity as the addressed store answering the address it was asked for; a question spanning many addresses shows the other face of it — the structure has no budget, so it neither fills one nor stops at one.
- **What each structure does when the relevant set does not fit, one line each.** The attribute arms, the interval store and the log do not choose: they return every address under the subject whatever the budget, and where that is more than fits, more than fits is what they return. The bounded document chooses by recency — the oldest text goes when the limit is passed — which is a rule with one constant, the limit itself, and on a question about a person what it keeps answers almost none of it. The shipped store chooses by rank, which is a ranking with three constants — the two weights and the half-life — and it pays for the choice: at the tightest budget its answer about a person falls by about a quarter, because a ranked list cut at a budget drops what the ranking put last. A ranking with constants nobody calibrated is what this programme disqualified when the question was arity, and it is the only mechanism in this table that answers the overflow question at all.
- **What has to fit grows with the span.** A subject spans about two addresses at the end of the first year and about seven and a half at the end of the tenth, and what an addressed structure returns for one grows with it, near enough linearly. The figure to read is that slope rather than the share inside a budget at year ten: a person with more attributes, or a span longer than a decade, reaches any fixed budget on the same line.

## Where the published designs came from

Ten structures were measured here before this round and every one of them was invented in this repository. The recommendation they produced had never met a published design on the workload that produced it. This section is what was searched, what it found, and why each design found is where it is.

**The sweep, and what it could not have covered.** Thirty-nine web searches and twenty-seven paper retrievals on 2026-08-16, over agent long-term memory, memory operations, temporal knowledge graphs for agents, forgetting and consolidation, conversational-memory benchmarks, and the named systems each of those returned. It is a sweep and not a survey: it covers what those searches surfaced on that day and nothing else. Two gaps are worth stating because a later reader cannot see them. Nothing submitted in July 2026 surfaced at all, and the newest item found carries an August 2026 identifier with a June submission date, so the weeks before this round are under-covered. And mechanism was read from the method section for four designs and from the abstract alone for the rest, so a design listed below as expressible has not been checked at the depth a port would need.

**What the seam withholds is the sorting criterion.** `harness/frame.go` is `Observe`, `Context` and `Size`. It names no query, no limit, no score, no similarity, no session and no model. A design needing one of those is not thereby excluded — every frame ported here takes a query from the turn and a limit from the shipped budget, and each design's own section says so — but a design whose distinguishing step *is* one of those cannot be ported without standing something in for it, and a substitution measures the substitute.

**Ported, and measured in every table above.** Generative Agents and its reflection mechanism (Park et al. 2023, arXiv:2304.03442), MemoryBank (Zhong et al. 2023, arXiv:2305.10250) and Mem0 (Chhikara et al. 2025, arXiv:2504.19413). Each has a section of its own stating what it needed and what stood in for it.

**Already in this record under another name.** Two designs converge with structures invented here, which is a result rather than a coincidence.

- **Zep / Graphiti** (Rasmussen et al. 2025, arXiv:2501.13956) holds facts as graph edges on two timelines and, when a new fact contradicts an old one, sets the old edge's invalid timestamp instead of removing the edge. That is `interval`: a value written with a start, closed rather than deleted when it stops being true, and answered as of the moment it is asked. The two were arrived at independently — the interval store is #457's — and the rows `interval` and `interval-declared` post are what that mechanism scores on this workload.
- **Mem0's graph variant** marks obsolete relations invalid rather than deleting them, which is the same move inside the design ported as `mem0`. It is therefore `interval-declared`'s store with `mem0`'s identity step, and this record measures each half separately rather than the combination.

**Could not be expressed through the seam, and what each needed.**

| design | what it needed | what the seam gives instead |
|---|---|---|
| MemGPT (Packer et al. 2023, arXiv:2310.08560) | the model to issue its own paging calls between a bounded context and a searchable archive | nothing decides when to page, and without that decision the design is `document` with a store beside it |
| A-MEM (Xu et al. 2025, arXiv:2502.12110) | three model calls per write — write the note's keywords and context, choose which neighbours to link, rewrite the neighbours it linked | with all three removed what is left is the memory stream `generative-agents` already measures |
| SeCom (Pan et al. 2025, arXiv:2502.05589), Nemori (arXiv:2508.03341), SimpleMem (arXiv:2601.02553), ES-Mem (arXiv:2601.07582) | a segment or session boundary to build memory units at | a turn, and nothing that says where one conversation ends |
| Memory-R1 (arXiv:2508.19828), AgeMem (arXiv:2601.01885), AtomMem (arXiv:2601.08323) | a policy trained by reinforcement learning to choose the memory operation | the operation, stated by the write, which is what `attribute-declared` and `mem0` already take |
| EM-LLM (arXiv:2407.09450) | the model's key-value cache | text in and text out |
| Letta's sleep-time agent (arXiv:2504.13171) | an idle phase in which a second agent rewrites the memory | a turn-driven loop with no idle phase in it |

**Expressible and not run this round.** Each would test something this record has not tested, and each is named so the next round has a list rather than a search.

- **MemoryOS** (arXiv:2506.06326, EMNLP 2025) — three tiers, each with a published hard capacity, and a heat rule promoting a segment once its visits, its size and its recency sum past a threshold. It is the only design found that carries its own capacity and its own eviction rule, which is exactly what §What a constraint costs puts outside every frame so that all of them are bound alike. Running it means either disabling the mechanism that distinguishes it or exempting it from that comparison, and which of those is right is a question this round did not settle.
- **FadeMem** (arXiv:2601.18642) — `memorybank`'s curve with relevance, access frequency and temporal pattern modulating it. It is the later version of a design measured here, so running both would say what that iteration bought.
- **ENGRAM** (arXiv:2511.12960) — episodic, semantic and procedural records behind one retriever, offered as the claim that the elaborate machinery is unnecessary. The shipped frame already types a turn the first two ways, so this is the null hypothesis stated as a design.
- **Hippocampus** (arXiv:2602.13594, MLSys 2026) — the same content under a compressed index and no model on the write path. It changes what a read costs rather than what a store holds, and this record measures that column in units rather than in time.
- **MemInsight** (arXiv:2503.21760, EMNLP 2025) and **LongMemEval's key expansion** (arXiv:2410.10813, ICLR 2025) — enriching the index at write time against enriching the query at read time, a separation no run here has made.
- **HippoRAG 2** (arXiv:2502.14802) — triples in an open graph with a personalized-PageRank read. `memory-architecture/` measured that multi-hop retrieval matches single-hop where sessions are independent, so this would put the published successor of that finding on this workload rather than open a new question.

### What a similarity-located write has to separate

A structure that computes an address knows which memory a write lands on. A structure that searches for it has to tell the memory the write updates from every other memory it holds, and whether that separation exists is a property of the workload rather than of any design's score. It is read here the way §The arity question read the timing rule, and for the same reason: a design whose mechanism cannot work on this workload should be shown not to work before its row is read as a score.

The candidate set is every value written so far, which is the widest a design could be holding and therefore the hardest separation to make. A design that prunes holds a subset of it, and a separation that fails here does not become available by holding less. A first assertion at an address has nothing to be separated from and is not counted.

**What these figures say.** The separation does not exist. The value already at the address a write lands on and the nearest value at any other address are the same distance from that write, within intervals that overlap, and for nine assertions in ten the nearest value in the whole store is at a different address. No gate separates them: set it low and every write ends whatever happens to be nearest, set it high and no write ever updates anything, and the two arms of `mem0` are those two ends rather than two settings between which a better one sits. Nearly every write's own target falls below the constant this project's store supersedes at, which is a fact about that constant and not the point — the point is that the two columns do not separate at any constant.

The reason is what a decade puts in a store. Two facts about one subject and attribute share two of the three things a rendered fact says; two facts about different subjects, drawn from one vocabulary of subjects and attributes and values, share about as much. Under a hosted embedder the absolute numbers would move; whether they would separate is #435's question and not this one, and the record does not claim they would not.

| quantity | value |
|---|---|
| assertions landing on an address already holding a value | 240 |
| similarity to the value at that address | 0.515 ±0.007 |
| highest similarity to a value at any other address | 0.509 ±0.008 |
| assertions whose nearest value is at another address | 217 |
| gate a write accepts a candidate at | 0.6 |
| assertions whose own target falls below that gate | 220 |

## What a run costs

Established before the run, as issue #443 requires.

No frame calls a model. Every structure measured here decides what it decides from what it was given; the ones that rank a store by similarity — the shipped frame, the three memory streams and the two Mem0 arms — touch an embedder, and it is the deterministic offline one `cmd/gagent` defaults to. The frames, the workload and the error sweep are free, and the model-call count in the first table below is the check on that rather than an assurance.

**The published designs raised three counts in that table and moved no figure any of them is read on.** Turns observed and contexts produced are the turn count times the frames times the arms, so both rose with the frames added; the embedder counts rose because five of the new arms embed what they hold and what they are asked. None of the three is a figure about a structure, and every per-frame row committed before this round is unchanged.

**Four things in this record were paid for**, and all four are in a table of its own after every free figure: asking a model to declare the operation for each of the workload's assertions, asking it again several times over for the assertions whose declaration has a consequence, having it read every message of every conversation shape, and having it turn each of the finding study's questions into an address. Each is one call per question or per message and nothing else, on the cheapest model that answers without billing reasoning as output. Each refuses to start past its own call bound and to finish past its own cost bound, all eight stated in that table, because a bound discovered afterwards is not a bound. Every answer set is committed, so scoring them costs nothing and re-running the measurement does not re-buy them: only `-declare`, `-resample`, `-extract` and `-resolve` reach the provider, and the key reaches the process from the environment and nothing else. What the shipped OpenAI embedder costs the read path, and which conclusions may be quoted across the two embedders, is #435's measurement and is not repeated here.

**Paid calls are below messages read in the shape table, and the gap is the cache and not a discount.** Answers are cached on the message and the three shapes are read in one run against one cache, and most of what any shape says is small talk drawn from a single pool, so a message that recurs between shapes is paid for by whichever shape said it first and read from the cache by the rest. The calls column counts what was purchased and the messages column counts what was scored, which is why the shape read first pays for nearly all of its 800 messages and the two after it pay for a fraction of theirs. A run reading a cache an earlier run filled would report no cost at all, which is why the committed artifacts come from a run that started with the cache cleared, and each carries the price of the calls it made.

The figure that matters for the designs coming next is in the cost table below, and it is in tokens rather than money on purpose: a price list expires and a token count does not. A design that rewrites a bounded document with a model on every turn makes one call per turn for the whole span, and the table states what one run of one such design spends in prompt and output tokens. Multiply by the price of whatever model it uses at the time, and by the number of designs and seeds being compared.

## What this record does not say

- It does not choose the policy for a declaration that cannot be trusted. §What would be chosen today states the structure and the direction of error as settled and says why the evidence cannot settle this third one: taking a model's declarations and always replacing are a genuine trade, and which failure is cheaper is a property of what the agent is for rather than of anything measured here. A reader looking for that decision will not find it in a table.
- It does not say what a published design scores as its authors run it. Every port here is this record's reading of a paper's mechanism, behind this seam, on this workload, under this embedder. A figure here is not comparable to a figure in that paper and neither refutes the other; what is comparable is one ported design against another in these tables, because those were measured the same way.
- It does not measure the designs whose distinguishing step the seam withholds. §Where the published designs came from lists them and states what each needed, which is the finding rather than an apology for the omission.
- It does not measure a writeback that decides what is worth storing. Both arms hold extraction constant, so what a model is measured deciding here is arity and nothing else; whether a model writes anything at all unprompted is `memory-cycle/`'s subject.
- It does not say what a bound does in the text arm. The constrained runs are the oracle arm alone, because a structure holding nothing has nothing to evict, so what harder conditions do to an already-empty store is unmeasured.
- It does not measure hard deletion. The shipped store archives what it evicts because that is what it does; whether deleting instead would change an answer is not run, though #471 establishes that no query reaches an archived row, so only the bytes could move.
- It does not measure a forgetting rule in operation on the unbounded store. The rule the frozen share would feed removes nothing here because nothing qualifies, so whether it is safe is untested rather than established — a run in which a rule never fires says nothing about the rule.
- It does not measure a question about an earlier year. The interval store's extra bytes buy the ability to answer one, and the workload asks none, so what that capability is worth is still unmeasured — the same gap #457 reported, unchanged by this round.
- It does not rank the structures. `attribute-declared` is a ceiling rather than a design, several arms are the ends of a range rather than candidates, and the best-scoring rule measured is disqualified — so the order of the rows is not an order of preference.
- It does not say the attribute frame's storage advantage would survive persistence. It is measured as an in-memory structure with no file format, so what it stores is what it holds; a persisted form would add a page layout and an index the shipped store's figure already carries.
- It does not measure retrieval quality as such. That is `memory-retrieval/`'s subject, and the constants that decide it are #411's.
- It says nothing about latency. No run time is measured, and the frames differ so much in what they do per turn that comparing theirs would compare implementations rather than structures.
- It does not say what shape real conversation has. Three shapes are measured and a transcript is none of them, so the record says what each shape costs and not which of them a deployment would meet.
- It does not measure a question that has to be understood before it can be placed. Every question here names its subject and, where it has one, its attribute, so what §Whether a structure can find what it holds measures is a resolution step at its easiest. A question that only implies what it asks about is where such a step would be tested, and none is asked.

## Limits

- **A published design is ported, not run as its authors run it.** Each is a reimplementation of the paper's mechanism behind this seam, on this workload, under this embedder and with this record's substitutions for whatever the seam withholds. The comparison it belongs to is the one in these tables.
- **The step that distinguishes two of the ported designs is a model call, and both are substituted.** Reflection's synthesis becomes a concatenation of the window's fact-carrying sentences; Mem0's judgement about which memory a fact updates becomes a similarity gate, at a constant the paper does not state. Both rows bound what the mechanism's shape buys and neither measures what the model would add.
- **The importance term takes two values.** Generative Agents scores poignancy from 1 to 10 with a model. The stand-in here is the shipped writeback's two salience constants, so min-max scaling turns that term into a flag saying whether the turn asserted anything. A real poignancy would order records inside the fact-carrying half, and this one cannot.
- **The forgetting threshold is the harness's.** MemoryBank states the curve and not the cut, so where the cut sits is this record's choice and is named in `harness/stream.go`.
- **`mem0` is handed the address on the read side.** It queries with the address every addressed frame is handed rather than with the user's message, which is what holds the identity comparison constant and which also means it is measured on holding and updating and not on finding — the same limit §What the oracle arm covers states for the addressed frames.
- **The literature sweep is one day's searches.** §Where the published designs came from states the date, the counts and the two gaps it can see; a gap it cannot see is not listed there.
- **One seed.** Every stage is deterministic in it. A second seed would redraw which subjects, attributes and values the threads get, which is the variation the stated intervals already carry.
- **Values are unique tokens.** That is what makes crediting exact, and it is not how every real fact looks: two people can be allergic to the same thing. A workload whose values collided would need a credit rule that reads subject and value together, and this one does not test any structure's behaviour under that.
- **Crediting is by reading the injected text.** It is the only rule that can score a structure with rows and a structure with one document on the same footing. It credits a value present for the wrong reason — a frame that returned everything it ever saw would score perfectly on recall, which is why the stale figure is beside it and why context size is reported.
- **A checkpoint does not write.** Real questions are turns and a design could learn from being asked one. Here it cannot, so nothing scores by echoing the question.
- **The bounds are on units, and a unit is not the same thing in two structures.** A row of the shipped store carries a sentence, a vector and an index entry; a unit of an addressed store is an address and a value. Bounding the count therefore bounds very different amounts of memory, which the byte columns beside each row are there to show. A byte-bounded run would fall on the structures in a different order, and only the unit sweep is reported.
- **One eviction rule was run for the capacity sweep and one capacity for the rule comparison.** The two are not crossed, so what a different rule would do at a looser bound, or age-ordered eviction at a tighter one, is not measured. The rule comparison sits at the tight bound because a rule that cannot be told from another where nothing has to go is not being measured at all.
- **The rule told by the write is indistinguishable from lowest salience on this workload.** A turn carrying no fact is exactly the turn the shipped writeback would give the lower salience, so the two rules read one signal. A workload where a write marked some of its own facts disposable would separate them, and this one does not have that.
- **The frozen share is zero because the workload is small, not because a structure cannot freeze.** A decade of questions carries more into contexts than any structure here holds, so everything is reached. The number is a property of this workload's ratio of questions to facts, and a store that outgrew that ratio would report differently — which is what the extrapolation beside it is for.
- **The frozen share is counted over what a search can reach.** The shipped store's archive is excluded, because no query returns an archived row: counting it as held would make the figure incomparable with a structure that has no archive. What the archive costs is in the byte columns and is not in the frozen column, and those bytes are the clearest case of the criterion in the record.
- **The subject relation counts what is live, not what would be useful.** A value true anywhere on the probed subject counts as related whether or not a person would think it relevant to that question. It is the widest relation the workload can define without inventing one, and a narrower one would need a judgement the ground truth does not carry.
- **The doubt figures are one model's, three samples deep.** Whether disagreement tracks error was measured at three samples of each consequential assertion. More samples would sharpen it, a different model might be in doubt somewhere else, and neither is measured. What the figure supports is that most of this model's wrong answers are settled; it does not support a claim about models in general.
- **No rule was built on the doubt beyond the simplest one.** Joining where the samples split is the one policy measured, because it needs nothing from the model but another answer. A rule weighing how many samples disagreed, or how far apart they were, would be a threshold on a number, which this round declined to build rather than tested and rejected.
- **One model, one prompt, one day.** The declaration figures describe the cheapest model at the provider this project ships, asked one way, on one afternoon. A larger model, a prompt that showed it examples, or a second attempt at the same call would all move them, and none of that is measured. The provider is not reproducible to the last bit either, which is why the answers are committed rather than re-bought.
- **The paid measurement asks about a sentence and not about a turn.** The model sees the address, what is held there, and the statement — what a writeback with a working recall would see. It does not see the conversation around the statement, and a real writeback would. Whether that context carries the arity a qualifier only implies is the obvious next question and it is not this one.
- **The workload has few writes that join.** Only the two coexistence classes' second values ever do, so the destroying direction's sweep has coarse resolution at the low end and its rows below that resolution show no errors rather than few. A workload with more coexistence would sharpen that curve, and changing it would stop every figure measured against this one from being comparable.
- **The interval store's distinguishing capability is unmeasured.** Every checkpoint asks about the present, so nothing here scores the one question a store of spans can answer and an addressed store cannot. A unit test asks it as of a moment inside a closed span and gets what was true then, which is what the claim rests on; the workload asks no such question and was not widened to, because a workload changed mid-programme stops every figure measured against the earlier one from being comparable. It is a separate issue and it would have to run against every frame from the start.
- **The byte figures understate the stores that keep instants.** Content and stored bytes count the text a frame holds. The interval store keeps two instants bounding every span and the addressed stores keep one beside every value, and neither is in the byte column — so the comparison of bytes between them understates the interval store by exactly the bounds that are the reason to use it. The units column carries that difference and the byte column does not.
- **What the oracle arm covers narrowed when the question arrived extracted.** A frame handed the address is measured on holding and updating and not on finding, and every figure outside §Whether a structure can find what it holds was produced that way. The shipped frame is measured on all three throughout, because it queries with the question's text, so a recall figure of its from those tables is not doing the same job as an addressed structure's. The finding half is measured in its own run, against its own file, and the two are not read against each other.
- **The questions name what they ask about in so many words.** Each is written from the attribute's own template, so `Which clinic does X go to?` carries the word the address is keyed on, and the multi-address question names its subject. Turning such a question into an address is the easiest form of that problem: the resolution figure is a ceiling on it and not an estimate of what a question that only implied its subject or its attribute would cost.
- **One phrasing of the multi-address question, asked of every subject at every year.** Nothing here says what a differently-worded question about a person would reach, and a question about a person that named a topic would span fewer addresses than one that named none.
- **Overflow is measured against the read budget and against nothing else.** Inside the budget means the answer fits the token budget every other figure in this record is measured at. Whether a model reads an answer that fits is a different question and is not asked.
- **No structure was given a way to choose what to leave out that it did not already have.** Where the relevant set does not fit, the structures that hold facts return all of it and the record reports that rather than a truncation: any ordering over the addresses under a subject is a mechanism those stores do not have, and building one to publish a number is what this programme refuses. What such a mechanism would cost is therefore unmeasured, and §Whether a structure can find what it holds names where it would have to live instead of scoring it.
- **The active index never approaches capacity.** Retention removes the observations before the ceiling is near, so capacity eviction never fires in either arm and this workload says nothing about what it would do.
- **The oracle never errs.** It extracts every fact exactly and nothing else. A partial or noisy extractor is the realistic middle of the bracket and is not measured.
- **Every figure but the shape study's is one conversation shape.** The decade run's turns are short single-clause sentences, one fact to a message, and a year's assertions scattered uniformly through it. §What harder language costs varies the sentence and the assertions in a message on a separate, smaller workload and re-measures nothing in the decade run. Sessions are single-day in all of them, and a workload where facts arrive in bursts would move both the token budget and the retention window's effect.
- **The shape study is smaller than the decade run, and no figure of one is comparable to a figure of the other.** Every message is read by a model once per shape and the bound on that sets the size. Where its plain shape differs from the decade run on what looks like the same quantity, the size is the reason, which is why the plain shape is in the study at all.
- **Three shapes are three points, not a sample of conversation.** One subordinate framing drawn from six templates, one packing width, and small talk that never changes. A transcript has neither a fixed width nor six frames, and nothing here says where between these three it falls, or what a fourth shape would do.
- **The extractor is handed the address space.** It is given the attribute names, so what is measured is whether a model finds the facts and puts them in the right places among names it was given. An extractor that has to invent keys and keep to them is a harder problem and is not measured; every misaddressing counted here is a choice among a dozen names.
- **The extractor reads one message and nothing around it.** No earlier turn, no store it is writing into, no answer it gave a minute ago. A real writeback sees all three, and whether the context that a packed message's neighbours provide would recover the decisions lost inside it is the obvious next question and not this one.
- **One model, one prompt, one pass over each message.** The extraction figures are the cheapest model at one provider, asked one way, on one afternoon, once. A second pass would move them — the shapes already fail to order in the extracted arm, which is what a sample this thin looks like — and the answers are committed rather than re-bought for the same reason the declarations are.
- **The shape study measures no bound.** It runs unconstrained, so what harder language costs a structure that must also evict is uncrossed with §What a constraint costs and unmeasured.
- **The results are not regenerated by `make ci`.** A full run is minutes of CPU, so CI compiles the harness, runs its tests, and checks this record against the committed results; it does not re-measure. A change in `internal/memory` that moves these figures will not fail CI, and re-running is a manual step.

## Reproducing

- `go run ./docs/experiments/memory-frames/harness -seed 7` — the decade measurement and the tables in this record. Offline, no API key, no cost.
- `go run ./docs/experiments/memory-frames/harness -harder -seed 7` — the conversation shapes, scored against the committed extractions. Offline, no API key, no cost.
- `go run ./docs/experiments/memory-frames/harness -finding -seed 7` — the finding study, scored against the committed resolutions. Offline, no API key, no cost.
- `go run ./docs/experiments/memory-frames/harness -render` — rewrites every generated region of this record from the committed results without measuring anything.
- `go test ./docs/experiments/memory-frames/harness` — the workload's ground truth, the credit rule, the seam's frames, the interval, the determinism check under two scheduling conditions, the shapes' framing against the cue rule, the extraction score's three failure kinds, the question forms and the finding study's agreement with the committed decade rows, and the check that this record matches the committed results.
